#2802. Fantasy Football 2026: Waiver Wire, week 10 edition

Welcome to the Fantasy Football Waiver Wire for Week 10.

We're at the halfway point of the NFL season, and for most fantasy leagues, only five weeks remain before the playoffs start. A critical stretch of the season is here, and with the bye weeks still in play and lots of players battling injuries, the Waiver Wire continues to be an important resource, even if it's mostly been picked clean in the last month or so.

The NFL trade deadline is Tuesday at 4 p.m., and whether certain players (Jonathan Taylor, Tony Pollard, George Pickens, Josh Downs, Brian Thomas, Jr., Dallas Goedert, and a few others) get shipped will have an impact on waiver moves. Pay close attention to trade news.

The Process: Each week, I try to identify the top Waiver Wire targets and evaluate the short- and long-term prospects of those players. I also do my best to reveal which players who are coming off a big week are fool's gold. Finally, I look at streamers who might help you in a pinch (this is usually more important during Bye weeks).

Some players discussed won't be available in your league. I try to list players who are available in more than 50% of leagues. But what does that even mean? A 10-team league with six bench spots will have a much larger Free Agent pool than a 14-team league with eight. Most leagues fall somewhere in between. This isn't "one size fits all." By way of example, I won't list Bryce Young, Kirk Cousins, Tyler Allgeier, Devin Singletary, Jordyn Tyson, or Ryan Flournoy, among others who probably aren't available in any of your leagues.

Note that I'm writing this column BEFORE the Week 9 Monday night game (MIN@BUF).

WEEK 10 BYES: Bears, Broncos, Buccaneers, and Eagles

Geno Smith makes my list once again. I think that makes five straight weeks. He struggled against the Chiefs, as expected, in a game played without Garrett Wilson (concussion). The Bills are up next, and Geno will be forced to play catch-up in that contest. The Chargers and Dolphins follow. Geno continues to be a viable play as a QB2.

Aaron Rodgers has been decent for fantasy this season and a mainstay in my QB waivers list. Coming off the bye this week, the Steelers get the red-hot, high-scoring Bengals. Rodgers threw for almost 300 yards and three TDs against Cincinnati in Week 6. He should be getting some more receiving help soon, and is a nice streaming option this week and beyond.

The QB2 options have thinned out due to injuries and poor play. Here's a telling statistic: 47 QBs have started games through Week 9 and that's the second highest total this century. There are quite a few available "starters" that I wouldn't look to add, either because they don't offer much upside (Joe Milton III, J.J. McCarthy, Gardner Minshew II, any QB on the Chargers) and/or it's not clear how long they'll be the starter (Shedeur Sanders, Drew Lock).

I'm curious to see how Carson Wentz plays on Monday night, and he could be one to consider if you're desperate. I have him ranked ahead of the guys I just listed.

Note that McCarthy and Lock are not locks to be healthy enough to play this week.

Need a Week 10 streamer? Jacoby Brissett might be available in your league. I'm not a big believer, but he should be ready to start for the Ravens in Week 10 and if you're stuck, you could do worse than a date with the Chargers, who gave zero effort on defense against the Texans.

I don't think the Raiders are trading Kirk Cousins or benching him any time soon. Stashing Fernando Mendoza isn't worth the roster spot at this point.

The trade deadline figures to have the biggest impact here. Seth McGowan is apparently still available in more than half of leagues on some big platforms. If Jonathan Taylor gets moved, his value shoots up. Even if he stays put, McGowan is getting more work and is one of the better handcuffs to roster.

The same is true for Tyjae Spears and Nick Singleton on the Titans, if Tony Pollard gets traded.

Corey Kiner stands to be the Week 10 starter for the Patriots if Rhamondre Stevenson misses time. Stevenson left Sunday's blowout loss to the Packers with a knee injury (plus, he lost a fumble), and TreVeyon Henderson missed the game with an illness. The Patriots only have one game before their Week 11 bye, so I wouldn't go too crazy with a bid for Kiner, but he's going to be a very popular add this week.

Audric Estime is another back who could get more run as the de facto "starter", but it could be short-lived, depending on when Travis Etienne, Jr., is back. Estime ran well as the "early downs" back on Sunday (15-72-1), while Alvin Kamara got most of the third down and passing game work. He'll be a competitive add this week, as this is a good offense and Etienne's imminent return isn't assured. This week's matchup (CAR) is a juicy one.

Kaleb Johnson and Marshawn Lloyd are worth adding, with Josh Jacobs possibly headed to IR. Green Bay can't run the ball, but volume is volume. Lloyd missed Week 9 with a knee injury so I'd prioritize Johnson.

Backs who should be rostered: Kenneth Gainwell, Dylan Sampson, Emmett Johnson, and Kaelon Black.

Jonathon Brooks (IR stash) is another to consider if you have an IR spot.

DeMario Douglas is filling the void created by A.J. Brown and Romeo Doubs being out of the lineup, and he's my top choice at WR. The volume is inconsistent, but he's quietly put up three Top-10 weekly finishes at his position over the past five weeks.

I'll recommend another guy with the same last name. Miami's passing offense is bad, but Caleb Douglas is emerging as their top target, and a decent downfield threat. He's put up between 70-95 receiving yards in three of four complete games he's played.

Kayshon Boutte and Tank Dell both went off in the Texans' rout this week, but I'd be careful chasing those performances too hard. Both receivers are boom/bust deep threats, and the Chargers didn't show up at all this week.

The 49ers and Rams continue to lose receivers like I lose my eyeglasses. I always eventually find them, though. These two teams have struggled to find replacements who can step up. It's hard to recommend much here, but I think you can consider Jordan Watkins, De'Zhaun Stribling (IR stash), and with Puka Nacua now joining Davante Adams on IR, one of Konata Mumpfield or Tutu Atwell is likely to step up (and keep an eye on the Rams trying to add a receiver via trade).

Need depth? I'll repeat a bunch of names I've listed in recent weeks, together with some new ones: Antonio Williams, Pat Bryant, Keenan Allen, Jahan Dotson, Jakobi Meyers, Savion Williams, and Chimere Dike.

Mike Gesicki finally didn't score last week. He's very TD-or-bust, and so is Pat Freiermuth. If you need a replacement this week, I like both as streaming options (their teams face each other).

Darren Waller went off in Week 9, after vanishing for more than a month, so he'll be a popular target this week. It's buyer beware, but an 8-90-1 line on 10 targets will always turn heads. Just know that you might get one catch for four yards next week.

Chig Okonkwo has been in this column for a solid month now. He hasn't popped but has been serviceable.

Terrance Ferguson was expected back last week but wasn't activated. Hopefully his ankle will be OK this week, as the Rams will be without Nacua and Adams for at least the next month. Ferguson will compete with the other tight ends in L.A., but when all is said and done, he should be the best of the bunch, in an offense that's going to have to feature them.

That's all I've got. Good luck with your waiver claims!

#2801. Reds' Sal Stewart wins NL Rookie of the Year after 112 RBI season

Sal Stewart never took a day off this season. He played all 162 games for the Cincinnati Reds, hit 32 homers and drove in 112 runs. No Reds rookie had ever driven in more.

Monday, it made him the National League Rookie of the Year.

"Man, relieved," Stewart said on MLB Network after winning the award. "I told myself I knew this award was going to find me, because that's how I was raised. I worked hard and I did what I could.

"I'm a kid from Miami. I worked hard for this moment. A lot of people doubted me."

The 22-year-old first baseman received 20 of 30 first-place votes and 170 points from the Baseball Writers' Association of America. New York Mets outfielder Carson Benge was second with seven first-place votes and St. Louis Cardinals second baseman JJ Wetherholt was third with three.

Stewart is the ninth Reds player to win the award. Jonathan India was the last in 2021.

Cincinnati gets a draft pick out of it. MLB's Prospect Promotion Incentive rewards teams that give top prospects a full rookie season in the majors, and the NL winner earns his club an extra pick after the first round of the 2027 draft.

Stewart spent most of the summer as the best run producer in the sport. On Aug. 26, he became the first player in the majors to reach 100 RBIs. He finished third in baseball, behind Alec Burleson's 116 and Pete Alonso's 113.

2018 AL – P/DH Shohei Ohtani, Los Angeles Angels

2018 AL – P/DH Shohei Ohtani, Los Angeles Angels

By the end of April, Stewart had nine homers and 29 RBIs, was hitting .281 and had gone 7-for-7 stealing bases.

May and June were harder. He hit under .250 in both months.

He fixed it in July and was named an All-Star. In August, he won NL Rookie of the Month for the second time, the first Reds player to win it twice in one season.

The Reds kept finding spots to keep his bat and speed in the lineup. Stewart appeared in 117 games at first base, 45 at third, 11 as the designated hitter and eight at second base.

Jim Greengrass drove in 100 runs as a Reds rookie in 1953. His record held up for 73 years.

Frank Robinson hit 38 homers as a rookie in 1956. Until this year, he was the only Reds rookie with 30. Stewart is the first to do it with 100 RBIs. His 20 homers before the All-Star break were another franchise record.

He finished with 164 hits, tied for second in the NL, the most by a Reds rookie since Pete Rose had 170 in 1963. He led all NL rookies in homers, RBIs, extra-base hits (57) and total bases (285).

No Reds rookie had played every game since the NL moved to a 162-game schedule.

Only one other rookie has put up 30 homers, 100 RBIs and 15 steals in a season: Jose Canseco in 1986.

Pat Zachry, pitcher, 1976 (shared with San Diego's Butch Metzger)

Not everyone thought Stewart was the best rookie in the league.

Stewart had the power numbers. Benge and Wetherholt had the better all-around seasons.

Benge hit .282 with 20 homers, 65 RBIs and 22 steals for the Mets. He was the better hitter for average, the faster runner and better fielder.

Wetherholt, the Cardinals' second baseman, didn't come close to Stewart at the plate. He hit .242 with 17 homers. But he was a Gold Glove finalist and one of the best defenders in the league.

That's why both rated well ahead of Stewart in WAR. FanGraphs had Benge at 4.3 and Stewart at 1.9. Baseball Reference had Wetherholt at 4.5 and Stewart at 1.8. Stewart's defense at first and third base cost him.

Stewart hit .247 over his final 24 games, with one extra-base hit and six RBIs. Alec Burleson passed him for the NL lead in RBIs in the final week.

The voters still went with the homers, the RBIs and the 162 games.

2025: Drake Baldwin, catcher, Atlanta Braves

2024: Paul Skenes, pitcher, Pittsburgh Pirates

2023: Corbin Carroll, outfielder, Diamondbacks*

2022: Michael Harris II, outfielder, Atlanta Braves

2021: Jonathan India, second baseman, Cincinnati Reds

2020: Devin Williams, pitcher, Milwaukee Brewers

2019: Pete Alonso, first baseman, New York Mets

2018: Ronald Acuna Jr., outfielder, Atlanta Braves

2017: Cody Bellinger, first baseman, Los Angles Dodgers*

2016: Corey Seager, shortstop, Los Angeles Dodgers*

This article originally appeared on USA TODAY: Reds' Sal Stewart wins NL Rookie of the Year after 112 RBI season

#2799. Miami reportedly to host NBA All-Star Game in 2028 for first time since 1990

The NBA All-Star Game is returning to Miami.

Per ESPN's Shams Charania, the NBA is finalizing Miami as the host city of the league's 2028 All-Star Weekend.

Despite being a prime destination for a February party, this will mark just the second time that Miami has hosted the NBA's All-Star weekend. The first and only other time was in 1990, the second season of the Heat's existence.

Magic Johnson won MVP in that game, while David Robinson, Reggie Miller, Dennis Rodman and Scottie Pippen all made their All-Star debuts.

Miami will host the NBA All-Star Game in 2028 for the second time.

Since Miami last hosted, 10 NBA cities have hosted multiple All-Star weekends: Los Angeles (4 times), New Orleans (3 times), Atlanta, Charlotte, Cleveland, Houston, New York, Orlando, Phoenix and Salt Lake City (2 times each). The Bay Area has also hosted twice since 1990 with games in Oakland and in San Francisco.

Phoenix will host again in February from the 19-21.

As currently iterated, the NBA All-Star weekend officially consists of the Rising Stars Game on Friday night, the skills contest, 3-point contest and dunk contest on All-Star Saturday and the All-Star Game itself on Sunday.

All in all, the 7-2 Bears are in a great position to continue the playoff push through the season.

#2797. Sal Stewart named NL Rookie of the Year

Reds first baseman Sal Stewart has been named NL Rookie of the Year, taking home 20 of 30 first place votes en route to a comfortable win. Stewart takes home the award on the back of a .263/.330/.457 season (112 wRC+) with 32 HR and 112 RBI, with 15 steals tacked on to boot. Carson Benge finished second, while Nolan McLean finished 4th.

Benge was named a finalist for the award alongside JJ Wetherholdt and Stewart last week. Thomas Henderson profiled each of those finalists earlier today, concluding that Stewart was the likeliest to win on the back of his 30 homer season. Indeed, Stewart has already been named Sporting News Rookie of the Year, a version of award voted on by players rather than writers. Earlier today ahead of the official announcement, betting odds (per Kalshi) had Stewart as a shoe-in for the official version of the award.

While it's easy to understand why Stewart has supporters (Dingerz, baby), his analytical case is quite poor. Wetherholdt and Benge were both far superior players by all WAR metrics, and even other rookies like TJ Rumfield and Joe Mack were more valuable overall contributors. A.J. Ewing, who tallied only 492 AB to Stewarts's 700, was nearly as valuable in his own right. At least three rookies on the mound – Logan Henderson, Andrew Alvarez, and Nolan McLean – were also more valuable players.

In prior years, this would've been a mild annoyance to debate in chat threads or on Twitter, but with the implementation of the Prospect Promotion Incentive (PPI) system there are actual stakes here. For the Mets, this is a frustrating and somewhat damaging outcome. Despite having two of the three best rookies in Benge and McLean, the org will not receive anything in the way of draft pick awards, a boon that would've greatly helped their eternally challenged draft pool.

#2796. Malik Nabers: Fox tried to "create a narrative" about me being a bad teammate

Giants wide receiver Malik Nabers took to social media on Sunday night to express his displeasure with how he was portrayed during the Fox broadcast of his team’s 19-11 win over the Eagles.

In a post to his Instagram story that was later taken down, Nabers took particular issue with Greg Olsen’s characterization of him. After quarterback J.J. McCarthy threw a pass behind Nabers in the fourth quarter, Fox showed Nabers gesturing with his hands and Olsen said he was letting the quarterback know to put the ball in front of him. Play-by-play announcer Joe Davis said Nabers’s “body language has been terrible” throughout the game with Olsen agreeing that it wasn’t good.

The broadcast also showed Nabers slamming his helmet on the sideline at one point and sideline reporter Pam Oliver discussed Nabers’s desire to have the ball come his way. Olsen went on to say that there is “a fine line” between receivers demanding the ball and being overly worried about your own touches that it detracts from the success of others. Nabers responded to what he believes was an implication that he’s a bad teammate.

“Y’all be trying [to] create a narrative on me soo bad, it’s actually crazy!! If you [don’t] like the way I play the game, keep the camera off me!! SIMPLE! But [Olsen] calling me a bad teammate when every team I’ve been on, there’s NOBODY I’ve played with has said dat bout me is crazy! My face the same way if I’m havin a good game, bad, winning or losing,” Nabers wrote. “If it’s a problem. 1. Keep the camera off of me. 2. Pray bout it! Simple.”

Nabers finished the game with five catches for 43 yards, which snapped a four-game streak with at least 111 receiving yards. He said in his postgame media session that he was frustrated with how the offense played at points in Philadelphia, but his frustration with Olsen and Davis might have been even greater.

#2795. Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double

Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers stayed unbeaten Saturday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena, earning a 127-116 win over the Bucks and improving to 3-0 on the season.

Joel Embiid had his 10th career regular-season triple-double, tallying 28 points, 11 rebounds and 10 assists.

On his 30th birthday, Jaylen Brown posted 27 points and five assists. Tyrese Maxey had 26 points. VJ Edgecombe scored 17 and grabbed 13 boards.

The Sixers' absences on the first night of a back-to-back included LeBron James (low back/sciatica management). Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said pregame that he did not know yet who would be available for his team's matchup with the Pistons on Sunday.

Here are observations on the Sixers' victory Saturday:

Dean Wade made his Sixers debut and started in James' place. He'd missed the first two games of the regular season with a left ankle sprain.

The 3-and-D forward scored the night's first points by a sinking a corner three-pointer on the opening possession.

Embiid also had a sharp start. He moved well, made good decisions and served as the main hub of his team's offense in the early going.

The Sixers' seven-time All-Star center has often enjoyed facing Myles Turner. He entered the night with an average of 29.4 points in 18 previous games against the Bucks big man.

Embiid did a little bit of everything in the first quarter, posting eight points, four assists and four rebounds over nine minutes. After snatching an offensive board, Embiid drained a fadeaway jumper to give the Sixers a 10-1 lead.

One of Embiid's assists was quite stylish. He found Edgecombe open beyond the arc with a behind-the-back bounce pass. Edgecombe knocked down the shot and wound up going 3 for 4 from three-point range in the first quarter.

The Sixers added rookie Labaron Philon Jr. to their rotation. Jabari Walker stayed in the mix, though he didn't enter until the 7:36 mark of the second quarter.

Philon played four minutes in his NBA debut and missed his lone shot attempt, a corner three. On defense, Philon allowed a couple of blow-bys off the dribble. He generally looked like he belonged on offense, albeit without any production in his brief run.

Maxey banked in a half-court heave at the first-quarter buzzer to put the Sixers up nine points. The Sixers couldn't carry forward any momentum, however, and began the second quarter poorly.

Adem Bona had another rough stint amid a subpar start to the season. After Anfernee Simons missed a transition layup try, Ryan Rollins' three lifted the Bucks into a 40-37 lead.

The dreadful stretch for the Sixers on both sides of the ball called to mind how they'd fallen into a 29-point deficit Thursday night vs. the Cavaliers before clawing out a comeback win.

The Sixers mustered just seven points in first six minutes of the second quarter and had many stagnant possessions that concluded with contested jumpers. The team's transition defense was dismal for much of the second period. Milwaukee had the game's first 19 fast-break points.

The Sixers regained the lead late in the second quarter. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope made two three-balls in the second and nabbed three first-half steals. Embiid assisted a go-ahead, and-one Walker layup.

Brown hit a three with 11.8 seconds left in the second quarter. Unsurprisingly, his variety of shotmaking skills were useful without James in the lineup.

Even when James or Embiid sit, the Sixers should have a simple advantage in terms of volume of stars over most opponents this year. None of Brown, Embiid or Maxey had the sort of off night that would have improved the Bucks' odds of a road win. They'd all passed the 20-point mark by the end of the third quarter.

Maxey notched another final-possession bucket, this time with a more conventional make. He canned a jumper from the right elbow with 1.7 seconds left in the third to extend the Sixers' lead to 97-87.

The Bucks made a late push in the fourth quarter. Kel'el Ware's put-back, and-one layup cut the Milwaukee's deficit to 117-114.

The Sixers ultimately handled business. Embiid reached his triple-double by assisting a second-chance Brown jumper and and Caldwell-Pope soon drilled a dagger corner three. Embiid even threw down a last-minute dunk for good measure.

For the second consecutive season, the Sixers have a 3-0 start. They got to four wins last year before dropping their first game.

#2794. Grading the trade: Jaguars making run at Super Bowl after landing Eagles DT Jordan Davis

The Jacksonville Jaguars made their third trade on Monday, and it was the biggest one yet — literally and figuratively.

The Eagles will ship massive defensive tackle Jordan Davis (6-foot-6, 336 pounds) to the Jaguars. In return, Jacksonville will fork over a 2027 first-round draft pick and a 2029 second-round selection to Philadelphia.

Earlier Monday, the Jaguars acquired safety Jessie Bates III from the Atlanta Falcons and defensive tackle Davon Godchaux from the New Orleans Saints.

How does Davis fit into the Jaguars' defense? What about the haul they got in return? Let's break down this massive trade.

If you thought the Bates trade was a sign that the Jaguars were going for it, the Davis trade left no doubt.

When DaVon Hamilton went down with a season-ending injury, it was viewed as a massive loss. Hamilton might not be a household name outside of Jacksonville, but he was valued highly inside the team's facility.

So what did the Jaguars do? They went out and got two defensive tackles to plug that hole — nearly 700 combined pounds between them. Think that will plug the hole up front?

The Jaguars might still have a top-10 rushing defense this season, but the Baltimore Ravens ran for a season-high 172 yards against them in their first game without Hamilton. That the game happened Thursday night likely allowed the Jaguars to recalibrate their needs over the weekend and strike quickly Monday. Three times, in fact.

They were not getting major interior help from the likes of Arik Armstead, Ruke Orhorhoro, Matt Dickerson and Albert Regis, and that's why the Jaguars added multiple players up front. The appeal of Godchaux is that he can play in multiple spots, and Davis also can move around a little, although they figure to hold down the nose between them. That likely leaves Armstead and Orhorhoro as the 3-techniques.

If you look at the Jaguars' remaining schedule, there aren't a lot of strong running teams left on it. However, if we're using the all-in framework, this deal was as much about what the Jaguars might face in the playoffs. Three of the top four teams by rush average reside in the AFC — the Pittsburgh Steelers, Buffalo Bills and Kansas City Chiefs — and the Jaguars might have to go through at least two of them to get to the Super Bowl.

Now comes the cost, and it's robust. Next year's first-round pick is likely to be in the bottom half of Round 1. The Jaguars hope it's the 32nd pick, which goes to the Super Bowl winner; if the season ended today, they'd be giving up the 25th overall selection. We can't project where the 2029 second-rounder will land, but teams often subtract one round for every year in the future, which makes it about a fourth-rounder for now.

When you put it like that, the cost doesn't sound quite as high, even if there are some conditions attached. Still, you have to appreciate a team willing and able to strike when the irons are hot.

The flip side: Davis has been ordinary this season — and at times invisible. The Jaguars clearly feel they can unlock his talent, or they wouldn't have paid so much. Lining up on a unit that features Josh Hines-Allen and Travon Walker should help, but Davis' paltry production (0.5 sacks, two QB hits, no forced fumbles or tackles for loss and a snap rate hovering around 50%) is concerning.

The Eagles signed Davis to a three-year, $78 million extension in March, yet Davis' salary cap hit for 2027 is a very reasonable figure at just over $8.5 million. This is an important element for the deal, with the Jaguars relatively tight against the projected cap for next spring. There's plenty of time to make it all work down the road.

For now, the Jaguars have their sights set on a Super Bowl.

The Eagles had let it be known that Moro Ojomo, the team's talented third-year defensive tackle, was not available for trade. And they kept to their word.

Instead, they dealt Davis, whom the team signed to a major extension along with Jalen Carter this offseason. The thinking was that Davis and Carter would be the starting pillars up front for years to come. That now has come to an end, but a Carter-Ojomo pairing now sounds appealing, especially with Davis performing at a disappointing level this season.

In addition to shedding salary, the Eagles also have continued adding to their impressive draft-pick nest egg. In 2027, they now own the Jaguars' first-rounder, the Vikings third-rounder, plus other teams' Day 3 picks, in addition to most of their own selections. In the 2028 draft, they own the Patriots' first-rounder and the Ravens' second-rounder. And now they're adding 2029 selections to the till.

With Jalen Hurts' career hanging in the balance following his blood clot diagnosis, the Eagles need that flexibility in case they must acquire another quarterback. That's the reality. Even if Hurts is eventually cleared to play, there's some long-term doubt about whether he remains the face of the franchise.

With the Eagles showing a willingness to deal assets, it appears that they're fully throwing in the towel on the playoffs, even with the NFC East nominally still up for grabs. That also impacts Nick Sirianni's future as he now coaches a team that has gone from possible Super Bowl contenders to more talent-poor in a hurry, especially given all their injury trouble.

It's a tough short-term pill to swallow for the franchise and a fan base used to competing every season, but the long-term payoff could be immense. The Eagles' to-do list this offseason grows, but the potential of how they can reshape the roster is equally as exciting.

#2790. Observations after well-balanced Sixers hold off Bulls

Still no losing streaks to speak of for the 2026-27 Sixers.

He's listed at a strong 237 pounds, doesn't mind contact whatsoever, hunts the ball, and has a knack for winning contested boards. Walker showed all of that Sunday. Very hard to overstate how well he rebounded.

The Bulls' defense continued to look like one of the worst in the NBA and the Sixers' well-balanced offense kept the scoreboard moving. Brown nailed a turnaround jumper to give his team a double-digit lead. Walker knocked down a top-of-the-key triple to extend the Sixers' advantage to 12 points.

The Sixers had two apparent injury scares in the second half. Brown limped to the sideline with 6:55 to go in the third quarter, but he stayed in the game. Edgecombe headed to the locker room after subbing out at the 9:08 mark of the fourth quarter. He returned to the Sixers' bench and ultimately returned for a final stint.

On the floor, the Sixers couldn't ease to victory. Bulls rookie Caleb Wilson trimmed Chicago's deficit to 116-113 by draining a three. Sixers head coach Nick Nurse called timeout.

The Sixers were efficient enough in the half court down the stretch.

Embiid made a put-back hoop and a pair of free throws. Maxey drilled a side-step three-ball after a wraparound feed from James. The Sixers converted their clutch free throws and secured a winning road trip.

“You brought up Malik Nabers,” Olsen said, “and you never know exactly what’s being said. It’s easy for us to sit here and interpret body language. When we asked [offensive coordinator] Matt Nagy about him, he said, ‘Listen, he’s very vocal. When he didn’t get the ball to start the year, he let everyone know it.’ He said, ‘Me and Malik have a good understanding right now. What’s perceived as being negative, what’s perceived as being a distraction, I think we both have an understanding of each other that allows it to coexist.’ And just certainly is something to keep an eye on.”

Not long after that, the booth threw to Fox sideline reporter Pam Oliver for more on Nabers.

“Yeah, you guys talk about his body language,” Oliver said. “I can tell you a little more about his mindset. I talked to him. And he told me, ‘I just demand excellence out of everybody around me. Anybody that has a hand in my career. Because you only get one shot. It’s your life, it’s your career.’ It’s fueled by a great sense of urgency in that hard-fought return to the field from that knee injury. Nabers said — told me, ‘I’m not going out there running all these routes not to get the ball.’”

Said Davis in response, “I totally get it. But when your teammate scores the go-ahead touchdown and you still have that look? Not great.”

“Yeah, listen,” Olsen said, “there’s a fine line. I played with a lot of really good wide receivers. A lot really good players. There’s never been a great wide receiver who hasn’t demanded the ball. That kind of comes with the territory. There is a few exceptions to guys that are just a different personality and a different approach.

“But there is a very fine line between being demanding, being a competitor, and all those great things. That’s great. It just can never cross that it feels like it takes away from the success of your teammates. So when your teammate catches a wide-open touchdown, you need to be the happiest guy on the field. You can’t still be worried about your targets. So that’s the fine line. He’s a talented, young, really good player. He’s just got to keep it in check.”

That same drive included a third-down throw to Nabers in the end zone. Nabers couldn’t make the reception, leading to a field goal.

“Listen, you want to demand the ball — you can’t throw that ball any better,” Olsen said after Nabers failed to catch the pass. “That’s a touchdown. You’re a premier player in this league. You’ve got an opportunity there, you’ve got to catch it.”

That’s the full extent of everything that was said on the Fox broadcast during the relevant exchange.

The problem is that too many outlets don’t type quotes precisely. Some will mash pieces of different sentences together. Even if the overall message is the same (and, frankly, it seems as if Davis and Olsen were being critical of Nabers, with Davis being more pointed than Olsen), the absence of accuracy opens the door for criticism of the message.

In this case, Olsen never called Nabers a “bad teammate.” However, Olsen explained that there’s a “fine line” between “being demanding, being a competitor, and all those great things” and “tak[ing] away from the success of your teammates.” Which basically is one of the hallmarks of being a bad teammate.

That’s why all outlets that are going to pass along quotes made on broadcasts should strive to get the quotes right. It eliminates any ambiguity as to what was said — and it prevents a subsequent attack regarding the accuracy of the quote.

Especially when the accurate quote tends to lead to the same place.

"Absolutely not," Sirianni said, via ESPN's Tim McManus. "That was a surprise to me, but absolutely not."

On Sunday, as Sirianni left the field, his right hand was moving around a bit awkwardly as he walked past fans and into the tunnel. That led to speculation about the middle finger gesture, though Sirianni never looked anywhere but forward and definitely didn't make a clear gesture. His demeanor on the walk in question never changed.

Sirianni has a history of yelling at fans, including an incident in which he apologized after shouting at a group of Eagles fans during a 2024 game with the Cleveland Browns. But Sunday's exit from the field wasn't even close to that.

The Eagles are entering their bye week, and will return Nov. 22 for a matchup with the Pittsburgh Steelers. They dealt defensive tackle Jordan Davis to the Jacksonville Jaguars on Monday, in return for a first-round draft pick next spring and a second-round selection in 2029. They now have a combined 10 picks in the first two rounds of the next three drafts, which should give them plenty of room to regroup after this season ends.

Until then, Sirianni will attempt to get the Eagles back on track during the final stretch of the season. He didn't rule out any coaching changes, either, but that's not his focus just yet.

"Right now we're going through the self-scout process," Sirianni said. "Guys are working on studies right now, and we're determined to figure things out and get on the right track, and that's what we're worried about right at this particular time."

#2787. Bills-Vikings game carries major significance for both teams

The Bills and Vikings have identical 0-4 records in the Super Bowl. They enter Monday night’s game with identical 6-1 records.

And the stakes are very high for both teams.

For Buffalo, which hopes to force the January road to SoFi Stadium through the open-air new venue in Western New York, it’s important to maintain distance between the 6-2 Jaguars and 7-2 Broncos. (The division is looking like the Bills’ to lose, with the Patriots now at 5-4.)

For Minnesota, their status as the last unbeaten team in the NFL could dissolve to being the last team out of the NFC playoff picture.

The NFC North, which now has a 20-3 record outside the division, has the Vikings at 6-1, the Lions at 6-2, the Bears at 6-2, and the Packers at 6-3. (Combined, they’ve lost as many games as the 0-8 Chargers.)

Last week, when the Vikings picked up their first loss of the season, the Lions, Bears, and Packers won. This week, the Lions, Bears, and Packers have won. A loss by the Vikings would conclude a two-week window in which the Vikings are 0-2, and the rest of the division is 6-0.

It gets worse. The 49ers, Seahawks, and Rams each won on Sunday. In the end, five playoff berths will go to the four teams of the NFC North and top three teams of the NFC West.

Two of those teams will be left out. And, frankly, the Vikings seem destined to be one of them.

Even with a 6-2 record, if they lose to the Bills. Because even though the defense is good enough to win a Super Bowl, the offense isn’t good enough to win a playoff game. It could be just bad enough to keep the team from making the playoffs.

It was one thing for the Vikings’ defense to not gripe when the end result of each game was victory. If the second game of a four-week gauntlet doesn’t go Minnesota’s way (the Packers and 49ers are up next), things could get even more awkward in the locker room.

The problem is that, despite plenty of speculation and chatter, the Vikings don’t seem to be inclined to pull the cord on the emergency option of trading for Daniel Jones or Kirk Cousins. The organization’s chronic “just good enough is just good enough” vibe remains palpable.

Which means it all comes down to Carson Wentz against the Bills.

Could Minnesota win? When Buffalo last visited Minnesota in 2018, the Vikings entered the game as 17-point favorites. The Bills, fueled by then-rookie quarterback Josh Allen throwing for 196 yards and a touchdown and rushing for 39 yards and two touchdowns (including a hurdle over linebacker Anthony Barr), won the game easily, 27-6. (Last week, Allen said the leap over a linebacker was the moment he realized “I do belong here.”)

Four years ago, the Bills were favored by 6.5 points over the Vikings. Minnesota won a crazy game in overtime, 33-30, with Vikings receiver Justin Jefferson making the catch of the century on a fourth-down, game-on-the-line play.

Tonight, in many respects, the season is on the line for the Vikings. Especially if they won’t be trying to upgrade the most important position on the team, which in recent weeks has been the weakest on the roster.

#2785. Observations after Sixers suffer blowout loss to Cavs, fall in NBA Cup

Observations after Sixers suffer blowout loss to Cavs, fall in NBA Cup originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers couldn't rekindle the magic of their comeback home-opening win over the Cavaliers.

They fell Friday night in NBA Cup action, suffering a 119-95 defeat in Cleveland. The Sixers dipped to 8-2 and the Cavs improved to 5-4.

The Sixers' top scorers were Jaylen Brown (20 points), VJ Edgecombe (19 points) and Tyrese Maxey (17).

The Cavs got 23 points from Donovan Mitchell and 22 from Evan Mobley.

The Sixers' injured players included center Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain). Cleveland was down big man Jarrett Allen (right knee contusion). Sam Merrill sat out the second half with a right elbow injury.

With Friday's loss, the Sixers are 1-1 in Eastern Conference Group B of the NBA Cup. Their next Cup game is scheduled for Nov. 25 against the Heat.

The Sixers will conclude their three-road trip Sunday afternoon against the Bulls. Here are observations on their blowout loss to Cleveland:

After a missed Merrill three-pointer, the Sixers steamed ahead on the fast break and Edgecombe opened the scoring with a slam.

The Cavs replied with a 15-0 run kickstarted by six straight Mitchell points. Much like in the Sixers' Oct. 22 win over Cleveland, they were many levels below their best in the first quarter.

The Sixers began 0 for 4 from three-point range, including two LeBron James misses. The Cavs were also the better team in transition and limited the Sixers' chances to attack in the open floor by taking excellent care of the ball in the early going. Edgecombe was called for his second foul with 7:34 left in the first quarter.

Meanwhile, Joel Embiid expressed his frustration about a couple of early no-calls. Embiid's larger problem was that he couldn't hit any sort of shot besides free throws.

He went 0 for 9 from the floor in the game. The seven-time All-Star finished with six points and seven rebounds.

Embiid has had a subpar shooting start to the year, especially from three-point territory — he's now 3 for 28 (10.7 percent) — but Friday was the kind of extreme night that's bound to happen here and there over the course of a long season.

Of course, it's notable that Embiid's been playing through right foot plantar fasciitis and the Sixers have been keeping a close eye on his minutes. We'll see how the team manages his availability moving forward and whether Embiid is able to bounce back next time out.

James didn't play anywhere near his finest game either. He had eight points on 3-for-12 shooting, eight rebounds, three assists and six turnovers.

As a team, the Sixers were 36.5 percent from the field and 25 percent beyond the arc.

Jabari Walker rejoined the Sixers' rotation as the backup center with Bona sidelined. Anfernee Simons, former Cav Dean Wade and Kentavious Caldwell-Pope all remained rotation fixtures.

With Walker and Caldwell-Pope on the court next to three starters, the Sixers cut their deficit to single digits in the second quarter. Walker threw down a fast-break dunk that cut Cleveland's lead to 48-41. He gave the Sixers some good defensive possessions when switched on to James Harden and provided his usual physical, high-effort work on the glass.

Edgecombe was fantastic in the second, sinking three triples, racking up 15 points and nabbing three steals.

Many second-year players would have a tough time finding a rhythm after encountering early foul trouble, but Edgecombe was great as soon as he checked back in and delivered a ton of valuable buckets in the Sixers' comeback push.

The Sixers pulled to within 66-63 when Maxey picked a steal and threw a pretty one-handed bounce pass to Edgecombe for a dunk.

With Embiid and James offering little as scorers, Brown hit several shots that helped the Sixers stay in the game. After his 30-point performance last time out against the Pistons, Brown reached the 20-point mark for a third consecutive game.

Still, the Sixers' offense never got rolling. The Cavs maintained their lead and extended it late in the third quarter. By early in the fourth, the Sixers trailed by over 20 points.

Embiid didn't play the fourth quarter and logged 25 minutes. He's had zero made field goals on just one other occasion in his career, back in Nov. 2019 against the Raptors.

Even with their tremendous first nine games, the Sixers knew they'd hit speed bumps throughout the year. They'll try to get back on track Sunday in Chicago.

#2782. SC Freiburg vs. Union Berlin preview: Freiburg look to continue title push against struggling Union

Ironically, the 640km separating Berlin and Freiburg im Breisgau is a pretty accurate representation of where the teams currently sit on the table: with a win, Freiburg can push their way back into 3rd place as they continue hunting for a UEFA Champions League spot and maintain their pressure on Dortmund and Bayern, while Union Berlin could potentially sink back down into the relegation zone depending on Schalke’s outcome.

Will we see a shock at the Europa-Park Stadion, or will we see the points gap between both sides continue to widen?

Let’s begin with the home side, SC Freiburg.

Freiburg have been on a burner to commence the 2026/27 season with 5 wins, 3 draws and a solitary loss only coming against champions Bayern München, along with a respectable +8GD (18 goals scored). In addition to this, Freiburg ensured their Pokal survival by staving off recently relegated Wolfsburg with a last-gasp winner by Keisuke Gotō. The team from Baden-Württemberg also had a healthy start to their Conference League campaign with 2 wins and 1 loss, their most recent result being a 1-0 away win against Trabzonspor.

To go back to the Bundesliga, Freiburg managed to overcome a 1-0 deficit to Augsburg to come away from Bavaria with a 2-1 win in their most recent match. A win against Union Berlin would see them not only move back up to 3rd, but also come to within 2 points of league leaders Borussia Dortmund and Bayern München.

Apart from their 3-1 loss to Bayern München, Freiburg have been otherwise flawless at home and look poised to strike while the iron’s hot as they push for a first-ever UCL qualification.

A duo to watch this match will certainly be the strike partnership of Igor Matanović and Yuito Suzuki, who account for 8 goals (4 goals each) - nearly half of Freiburg’s total goals this season.

SC Freiburg will be without Florent Muslija and Patrick Osterhage.

Ironically, the club known as “Eisern Union” (Iron Union) seem to have let their iron cool down as they sit near the bottom of the table as one of the league's most out of form teams.

Since shocking the footballing world by qualifying for the UCL in the 2022/23 season, Union Berlin has in fact looked quite malleable in recent seasons (I promise that’s the last metal-related joke I will make!), with this season shaping up to be a relegation survival battle for the Berliners.

With only 1 win in 8 games thus far (4D, 3L), Union squandered their previous Bundesliga matchup against 1. FC Köln at home, losing out on some much needed points with direct relegation rivals. This followed a 5-0 battering by that very same Köln team in the Pokal less than a week prior.

The bad news for Union continues if we look at their away form thus far - in just 3 matches, Union has drawn 1 and lost 2, with a shocking -11 goal differential (1 scored, 12 conceded), by far the worst in the league.

With local rivals Hertha Berlin having just beaten top-of-the-table rivals Wolfsburg and continuing their flawless run in the 2. Bundesliga (10W, 1D, and a +16 GD), the pressure is surely beginning to mount on Union securing their status as Berlin’s best team.

Union Berlin will miss the likes of Andrej Iic, Stanley Nsoki, Andrik Markgraf and Robert Skov. Felix Uduokhai remains out via suspension after his red card against Augsburg.

To give Union fans a bit of hope, Freiburg have had a shaky record in recent years against the team from Berlin.

Union have been flawless against Freiburg in their last 7 matches (4 wins, 3 draws, and outscoring Freiburg 9:4), with their last loss occurring nearly 4 years ago to the day. Union is also currently on a 2-game winning streak against Freiburg when playing in the Europa-Park Stadion.

This does come with a caveat however, which is that both sides were a lot closer in regards to form and table positioning in the same fixture last season - in 2025/26, Freiburg was 8th (34 points, -5 GD) while Union was 11th (28 points, -12 GD). This time, 16th place Union (7 points, -12 GD) will really have to punch up if they want to win against 4th place Freiburg (16 points, +8 GD).

If Union Berlin are to regain their form, perhaps they can make it happen against Freiburg and potentially eke out another win to pick up some much needed points and create some separation at the bottom of the table.

#2778. ‘Let's check the ball up': Jaylen Brown ready for first game against Celtics

'Let's check the ball up': Jaylen Brown ready for first game against Celtics originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Jaylen Brown was asked Sunday whether he and Jayson Tatum had spoken since the former teammates addressed their relationship during the preseason.

Does he plan to approach Tatum before Tuesday night's game at Xfinity Mobile Arena?

"I mean, he's on his side, I'm on my side," Brown said. "We're playing basketball. Both of us are trying to win. So it's nothing to talk about. Let's check the ball up."

After months of public comments, documentaries and questions about how their partnership ended, the basketball will finally take over.

Brown will face Boston for the first time since the Celtics traded him to Philadelphia for Paul George and four future draft assets in July. He spent his first 10 NBA seasons in Boston, played nine alongside Tatum and helped the Celtics win the 2024 championship, earning Finals MVP honors.

Tuesday's 8 p.m. game on NBC will also bring together the top two teams in the Eastern Conference. The Sixers are 9-2. Boston, riding a three-game winning streak, is 8-2 and sits a half-game behind Philadelphia.

"Another good opportunity for our team to be able to show what we're made of," Brown said. "I'm looking forward to it. Big-time matchup, two heavyweights in the East. Let's get it."

Brown has not hidden his appreciation for Boston, even as more has emerged about the deterioration of his relationship with Tatum and the organization.

"I love Boston," he said Sunday. "Boston's a great city. A lot of people I developed relationships with, you know what I mean? But I'm just focused on what's in front of me right now. And that's the most important thing."

He also acknowledged everything that accompanies an abrupt end to a decade with one organization.

"Decisions were made," Brown said. "It's life. It's business. Move on. It's OK. … It's emotions. You've got to pack up and move your stuff. Your family's got to pack up and move. But it's life. It's happened to numerous amounts of people, myself included.

"So I just look forward to being able to build with my team over here, trying to make the best of my situation."

Brown faced the Celtics during the preseason, but Tuesday will be the first meeting that counts. He said he did not know whether that would introduce emotions that were absent during the exhibition schedule.

"We'll see how I feel when we get out there," Brown said. "As of now, I've got no emotion toward nothing. So I'm just ready to go."

Tatum was similarly restrained after Boston's 117-97 victory over Milwaukee on Sunday.

"It's another game on the schedule," Tatum said. "It's the Celtics versus the Sixers. In the words of Brad Stevens, they all count as one."

Tatum called the Sixers "an incredible group" with a "great coaching staff," but declined to add to the discussion surrounding his relationship with Brown.

"Drama gets clicks and views," he said. "That's not my thing." CBS Sports reported Tatum's full comments Sunday night.

Celtics head coach Joe Mazzulla was even more concise.

"It'll be fun," Mazzulla said. "It'll be a good atmosphere."

Brown is not the only centerpiece of the July deal who will face his former team.

George has settled quickly into Boston's lineup, averaging 20.4 points and supplying the high-volume outside shooting the Celtics have long prioritized. He scored 27 points and made five three-pointers Sunday as Boston beat Milwaukee for its third consecutive victory.

The Celtics have not needed George to duplicate everything Brown provided. He has fit alongside Tatum, Derrick White and Boston's returning core as another willing shooter and versatile defender.

Tatum is averaging 27.1 points, six rebounds and 7.8 assists. His playmaking has been especially important for a team that replaced Brown's downhill pressure with more shooting and ball movement.

Boston enters Tuesday ranked third in offensive rating and fourth in defensive rating. It is one of two NBA teams currently inside the top five on both ends of the floor.

The Celtics have also made 15.6 three-pointers per game, the second-highest average in the league. They set an NBA Cup record by hitting 24 against Charlotte on Friday, then made 18 more Sunday in Milwaukee.

That presents a clear challenge for a Sixers defense that has generally performed well but has occasionally surrendered clean perimeter looks after committing extra help toward the ball. Tatum, George, White, Payton Pritchard and Sam Hauser can all punish late rotations.

The Sixers have reached 9-2 without depending upon the same formula every night.

Tyrese Maxey leads them at 26.9 points per game. Brown is averaging 22.3 points, 6.7 rebounds and four assists while shooting 47.7 percent from the field and 39.2 percent from three. Joel Embiid and LeBron James have supplied scoring, passing and defensive flexibility around them.

Brown has repeatedly described the team as unfinished despite its record.

"From our perspective, it's been some growing pains for sure, even though we've been able to win," he said. "The result, we're not so much fixated on the result. It's more so about the process, and we've got to improve our process and our mentality to creating a winning environment."

Boston should provide the clearest early measure of that process.

The Celtics play considerably slower than the Sixers, but their half-court execution has been more efficient. Philadelphia will want to create turnovers and run before Boston can set its defense. The Celtics will attempt to control the pace, generate three-pointers and make the Sixers execute against a defense that has yet to allow an opposing player to score 30 points this season.

There is also the unavoidable Brown matchup. Boston could use Tatum, George and White against him at different points, giving the Celtics several defenders capable of absorbing his drives without immediately sending help.

Nick Nurse expects Brown to bring something extra, even if Brown has publicly downplayed the emotional component.

"It's going to be very different for him," Nurse said. "I think probably a little bit of emotion in there, too, but also I think he's going to be very motivated to play."

Boston's rotation is healthy entering the matchup. Two-way guard Devin Carter is out because of an illness.

Dominick Barlow and Ariel Hukporti remain out for the Sixers. Adem Bona could return after missing Sunday's contest.

The betting market views the matchup as essentially a toss-up. Monday afternoon listings had the spread moving between Boston minus-1.5 and Philadelphia minus-1.5, depending on the sportsbook, with the total at 215.5.

That fits the larger picture. Philadelphia owns the East's best record. Boston has the conference's strongest point differential and has outscored opponents by 10.7 points per game.

The surrounding conversation will center on Brown and Tatum until the opening tip. Brown does not intend to contribute any ceremonial small talk.

"He's on his side," Brown said. "I'm on my side."

#2776. Aaron Glenn on giving up defensive play-calling: I'm looking at everything

Jets head coach Aaron Glenn took over as the team’s defensive play-caller this season and things got off to a pretty good start in September.

The Jets only allowed 30 points over the first two weeks of the season, but they’ve allowed 193 in the last seven games. That includes Sunday’s 35-point performance by the Chiefs and the Jets have only won one of those seven contests to drop to 2-7 on the season.

On Monday, Glenn was asked at a press conference if he would consider handing off the play-calling role to defensive coordinator Brian Duker in the second half of the season.

“Well, I would say this, and listen, we have not played really good defense the last couple of weeks, and I am very aware of that,” Glenn said, via a transcript from the team. “But again, there’s been evidence of us playing really good defense throughout this season and listen, I will never say never to anything, but I do know this: I’m looking at everything and I’m looking of ways of us to improve, and whatever that is for us to get better, you can best believe I’ll make those changes. And I think I’ve done that throughout my career to be able to do that is I’m not afraid to make a change. I’m not saying that is going to be the change, but I’m looking at everything.”

Given the Jets’ record, it’s hard to imagine too many little tweaks that are going to lead to a major change in circumstances for the team. It’s also hard to imagine Glenn doing much to improve his chances of sticking around after a 5-21 start to his tenure, but we’ll see if anything looks different when the Jets face the Bills at home in Week 10.

#2775. Jesse Minter does not address Lamar Jackson's injury, says he'll keep conversations with QB private

Over the weekend, reporting emerged that Ravens quarterback Lamar Jackson told teammates that he intends to return to play in 2026.

Baltimore head coach Jesse Minter, however, was not inclined to give any update on the star QB in his Monday press conference.

Jackson is reportedly out for 6-9 weeks with a fractured and dislocated thumb. He is currently on injured reserve.

Minter was asked what the chance is that Jackson will be able to return this season.

“You know, I’m going to keep most of the conversations between Lamar and I between Lamar and I,” Minter said. “Again, just like before, it’s a guy when you’re dealing with something, that no one wants to be on the field more. He’s probably going to do everything he can to put himself in position [to play]. But I’ll leave it at that.”

“I’m going to keep all that stuff between me and him,” Minter said.

Does that mean the public won’t know the nature of Jackson’s injury and if he can return?

“You may know, but that’ll come from him,” Minter said.

There was one more follow-up in the press conference, with Minter being asked if it’s accurate to say Jackson’s thumb was fractured and dislocated.

Minter chuckled before saying, “Again, that is specifics. I’ll keep it between me and him.”

Jackson has technically started five games this season. But he played just a half against Tennessee and threw only one pass against Buffalo before having to exit due to injury.

Jacoby Brissett is now set to take over as Baltimore’s starting quarterback with Jackson and Tyler Huntley on injured reserve.

#2774. Austin Reaves questionable, Quentin Grimes out vs. Hawks

#2773. Who won Jordan Davis trade? Grading Jaguars, Eagles in deal

In a day defined by the Jacksonville Jaguars' pre-NFL trade deadline dealings, the team's last move proved most consequential of all.

After completing two additional deals earlier in the day to boost their defense, the Jaguars agreed to acquire defensive tackle Jordan Davis from the Philadelphia Eagles in exchange for a 2027 first-round pick and a 2029 second-round pick.

The move served as a loud statement of purpose for both franchises. For the Jaguars, it was the strongest push yet in an ongoing effort to equip an otherwise stellar roster with everything it needs to make a title push. The Eagles, meanwhile, appear to be preparing themselves for a potential reset after rampant injuries and an ongoing seven-game skid dashed any hope of the organization remaining in the NFC's contending class.

But who made out better in the deal? Grades for each team:

With Monday's earlier trades to secure safety Jessie Bates III from the Atlanta Falcons and defensive tackle Davon Godchaux from the New Orleans Saints, the Jaguars had already made clear their belief in this group's ability to run with the AFC's best. But those moves seemed more about isolated opportunities rather than changing the course of the franchise.

This decision, however, belongs in its own category. In burning serious draft capital to land a monstrous presence in the middle, general manager James Gladstone is harkening back to his roots and having a full Los Angeles Rams moment.

But is Davis the kind of player who truly puts Jacksonville over the top?

Gladstone likes to talk about players who have "gravity." At 6-6 and 336 pounds, Davis certainly qualifies as one. Trying to properly account for him while also keeping edge rushers Josh Hines-Allen and Travon Walker at bay will be exceedingly difficult for any opponent. And the Jaguars simply haven't enjoyed rolling out this kind of force in the middle for quite a while.

Still, defensive tackles who fetch this kind of price tag typically offer far more against the pass than Davis does. His 6.6% pressure rate, while close to a career high, is nowhere near the likes of Jeffery Simmons and the other elite disruptors at the position.

There's a strong case to be made that Davis can be a force multiplier for Anthony Campanile's unit, which has proven to be plenty opportunistic in sustaining itself with pressure and takeaways. But Jacksonville is now without its first two selections in next year's draft. The Rams fueled their outlier approach to top picks by continually finding starting-caliber options – particularly at non-premium positions – in the middle to later rounds of the draft. Gladstone hasn't shown himself capable of that just yet. And given the uncertain state of the offensive line, that hubris could one day take a serious toll on Jacksonville's roster.

Jacksonville might have arrived at the same conclusion that other teams have in determining that a first-round pick can be worth the instantaneous returns from landing a top-tier defensive tackle. The Jaguars certainly paid as though they're getting such a player, who's already making top-of-market money. It's far from a certainty, however, that the bottom line will match the spend.

Regardless of whether Roseman goes any further in parting with veteran pieces, this was a shrewd value move.

And when it comes to deciding whether to pull the plug early on a marquee player, few NFL decision-makers have a better track record than the architect of the Eagles.

Davis was a sensible option for Philadelphia to keep when it was at the front of the pack in the NFC. With the team fading considerably and staring down major changes in the near future, however, the 2022 first-round pick became expendable.

This is the kind of return one might expect for a borderline elite defensive lineman. Davis, however, likely wasn't among the three most critical defenders on the Eagles' roster.

In the next three draft classes, Roseman is now equipped with 10 picks in the first two rounds. That gives him incredible flexibility to target a quarterback of the future as he sees fit and fast-track a return to relevance.

This article originally appeared on USA TODAY: Who won Jordan Davis trade? Grading Jaguars, Eagles in deal

#2772. Todd Monken not ready to name starter at QB for Week 10

Shedeur Sanders has given Todd Monken something to think about.

The Browns’ head coach told reporters in his Monday news conference that he’s not ready to name a starter at quarterback for the Week 10 matchup against the Texans.

That came after Jeremy Fowler of ESPN reported that Deshaun Watson would be back in the saddle after missing Sunday’s game.

“I really haven’t thought through it other than getting through this morning’s tape,” Monken said. “I just got done meeting with the guys. They’ll flush the game and then we’ll have a chance to meet offensively more [extensively] later today. We went through the tape, immediately had to break, go to personnel, then the team comes in, and we haven’t had a chance to circle back as an offensive staff.”

Watson started the first eight games of the season for Cleveland, generating five game-winning drives for the team’s five wins.

Sanders, who started after Watson missed the first two days of the practice week while in concussion protocol, completed 19-of-31 passes for 297 yards with two touchdowns and no picks against the Saints.

“Well I’m awfully proud of him,” Monken said of Sanders. “It’s been 10 weeks since he had reps with the ones. I think it showed up, the reps that we gave him in camp.

“I thought he battled. I thought he showed composure in the pocket, getting to his check-downs at times. He made plays with his feet in escaping. He made the scramble play to Jerry [Jeudy]. He made a big third-down conversion early and he made one right there in four-minute, right before the holding call that gave us a first down. So, that’s what you wanted to see.”

Monken noted that philosophically, he doesn’t really think of a player losing a job due to injury.

“It’s week to week,” Monken said. “A player doesn’t lose or win a job — I don’t know what that means. Each week, we put the best players on the field that give us a chance to win.”

Monken added that Sanders’ play didn’t necessarily alter his thinking about whether or not Sanders could play at some point this season.

“I was thrilled. I was happy for him,” Monken said. “Every player wants to go and play and play well. And there were moments [where] I didn’t coach well enough, he didn’t play well enough. But there were moments at the end of the game where he made plays, right? He got it in the players’ hands, did a really good job, was in command. So, I was really pleased for him. And I was hopeful he’d play well. I thought he had a great week of practice and there is no guarantee that will carry over. So I’m really happy for him.

“Does it give me confidence? From what I saw in the fall, you bet it does. It gives me more and more confidence that what I saw in the fall, there’s something to build on — for sure.”

We’ll see how that translates as the season continues. But the fact that Sanders played well enough to make it an open question does say something about his development.

#2771. Browns Get Burned by Player Released Just Days Before Week 9 Saints Game

Less than a week ago, the Cleveland Browns announced that they have released running back Audric Estime.

Before that, he saw action for a game this season with Cleveland, rushing nine times for 19 yards and a touchdown during a 13-10 win over the Baltimore Ravens at home.

Just days after he was waived by Cleveland, Estime found himself sharing the field with the Browns, this time as an opponent.

Estime reunited with the New Orleans Saints after his release from Cleveland, paving the way for the Saints to claim him off waivers.

On Sunday, he led the Saints' ground attack, rushing for 72 yards and a touchdown on 15 carries, as New Orleans defeated his former team to the tune of a 23-22 score via overtime at Caesars Superdome in New Orleans.

Nov 8, 2026; New Orleans, Louisiana, USA; New Orleans running back Audric Estime (30) scored a touchdown on a 2-yard run during the fourth quarter against Cleveland at Caesars Superdome. Mandatory Credit: Stephen Lew-Imagn Images

Estime's touchdown late in the fourth quarter put New Orleans in front, 16-13.

Cleveland responded with a game-tying field goal that forced overtime, where Saints tight end Juwan Johnson scored on a 15-yard reception. The Browns scored a TD in the next possession, but their two-point attempt failed.

This isn't Estime's first rodeo with the Saints, as he was signed in 2025 by New Orleans. New Orleans waived him in September before a stint with the Minnesota Vikings practice squad.

The Browns signed him off Minnesota's practice squad in October.

Interestingly enough, Estime outgained his former teammate and Cleveland's RB1 Quinshon Judkins, who had 43 rushing yards with zero touchdowns on 15 carries.

Cleveland is now 5-4 after losing two games in a row.

#2767. Week 10 Fantasy Football Waiver Wire: Seth McGowan, Corey Kiner among top pickups

Every Monday during the season, Justin Boone breaks down the recommended waiver-wire pickups.

Roster percentages are generated from Yahoo leagues. Free-Agent Budget (FAB) amounts are based on a $100 salary cap. Only players rostered in less than 50% of leagues are considered.

The quarterback position is very picked over at the moment. Malik Willis was featured in this spot last week, but he's now playing through a groin injury that's limited his abilities as a runner and neutered his fantasy potential. So, all that's left are uncomfortable streaming options.

The Steelers are coming off their bye week, which should help the 42-year-old Rodgers (turns 43 in December) feel refreshed for a date with the division rival Bengals.

Rodgers hasn't had many favorable opponents this season, but he did take advantage of his easiest matchup. That just happened to be against the Bengals in Week 3, when the veteran passed for 292 yards and three touchdowns en route to a QB8 finish.

In his last visit to Cincinnati, Rodgers threw four touchdowns back in Week 7 of the 2025 campaign. He's not a slam-dunk streamer, but he's a solid one given the options this week.

Desperation streaming options (if healthy)

Desperation streaming options (if starter is out)

Will the Colts trade Jonathan Taylor or not? The good news is that we'll finally have an answer to that question by Tuesday afternoon when the NFL Trade Deadline finally arrives.

There's been a variety of reports suggesting every possible outcome, including Taylor staying in Indy, so it's hard to project this situation. However, we'll know which jersey he'll be wearing before the waivers process on Tuesday night. I'll update this column once a trade occurs or the deadline passes.

For now, McGowan remains a must-stash. As long as Indy doesn't get a starting-caliber back as part of the return for Taylor, McGowan would be next man up to start — with DJ Giddens as the new change-of-pace option.

For as bad as the Colts offense has been lately, they still have one of the 10-best fantasy RB schedules in the second half of the season, so McGowan could be a sneaky fantasy RB3 down the stretch.

The elite backup RBs who are one-injury away from stardom should be rostered everywhere and I'll continue to highlight them in this space until they no longer qualify for this article.

It was clear early in the season that Johnson was one of the best backups to roster, in case anything ever happens to Kenneth Walker III. In recent weeks, Johnson's value has increased even more now that he's offering weekly flex production.

The rookie posted a fluky 128 receiving yards and a score in Week 8, but he followed it up with 34 yards and another touchdown on Sunday. Upcoming matchups with the Falcons, Cardinals and Bills are all spots where Johnson could be a viable flex play while also offering league-winning upside if Walker ever misses time.

Black is in the same situation as Johnson, you can basically just swap Walker's name with Christian McCaffrey's in the previous section.

The 49ers' rookie plays in one of the best offenses in the league and has flashed his potential with at least 60 rushing yards in three of his first eight games, while serving as CMC's direct backup. That includes the past two games, when Black turned 12 touches into 63 yards and a TD against the Falcons, as well as Sunday's outing, which resulted in 11 carries for a season-high 70 yards.

Next up is the Cowboys defense that's a top-five matchup for fantasy running backs. The 49ers are 4-point favorites in a game with the highest projected point total in Week 10 at 55.5.

Stashing a talented No. 2 back playing behind a 30-year-old starter who handled 450 touches last year is just an obvious move. Black should be rostered everywhere as a flex option with RB1 upside if McCaffrey were ever sidelined.

Rhamondre Stevenson left Sunday's game with a knee injury and was very quickly ruled out. Mike Vrabel didn't have an update Monday morning, but did acknowledge that his back was undergoing an MRI. With TreVeyon Henderson already sidelined indefinitely, that leaves Kiner as the next man up.

Though Kiner hasn't been asked to do much as a pass-catcher, the 2025 UDFA has shown some juice in the run game by topping 50 yards in three of his last five outings in a change-of-pace role.

If Stevenson can't suit up in Week 10, Kiner immediately becomes a high-end fantasy RB3 with the usual touchdown upside that comes with a starter. We can't lock that outcome in though until we get a proper update on Stevenson's injury.

Josh Jacobs fractured his finger and is set to undergo surgery that will keep him out a maximum of three games.

MarShawn Lloyd missed Week 9 due to a knee injury, which allowed Johnson to step up in Jacobs' absence. Johnson finished with 68 yards and a touchdown, making him the RB11 on the week with just MNF left to be played.

If you're wondering why Johnson isn't higher on this list, just take a look at Green Bay's upcoming schedule. The Packers face the Vikings this week, then have a bye before taking on the Rams.

So, even with Jacobs sidelined, this isn't the ideal setup for a spot starter. On top of that, we aren't sure if/when Lloyd will be available. If Lloyd is active in Week 10, it only further muddies the projections.

If Lloyd remains out, Johnson would be a risky RB3/flex versus the Vikings. If Lloyd plays, then both players would be risky flex options.

Chris Rodriguez Jr. at TEN (33% rostered)

Sione Vaki vs. NE (Germany) (2% rostered)

Dart-throw flex options (if starter is out)

DeMario Douglas has always been a risky receiver to put in your fantasy lineup due to his low floor. We witnessed that occur in Week 7, when he only delivered three catches for 23 yards.

However, Douglas has posted at least 12 fantasy points in his other four outings over the last five weeks, finishing as a top-10 WR three times. After hauling in five of his nine targets for 102 yards in Week 9, he currently sits as the WR23 with just Monday Night Football remaining.

Four touchdowns during this stretch have helped his cause, but he's come away with very respectable yardage totals in these games with at least 65 in each.

A.J. Brown's recovery timeline remains uncertain and with a bye looming, it seems likely we won't see him until Week 12 at the earliest. Fantasy managers can continue to take advantage by starting Douglas as a WR3/flex option with temporary upside.

Douglas has played four games this year that weren't impacted by injury. In those contests, the rookie has posted stat lines of 5-94-0, 2-70-2, 4-43-0 and 6-70-0.

With Malik Willis nursing a groin injury that's limited him as a runner, Douglas has seen 14 targets over the past two weeks.

When a first-year player is showing this kind of production early in his career, it's worth taking notice. Douglas can be started as a WR3/flex, especially given his intriguing matchups against the Colts and Bills the next two weeks.

After back-to-back games with at least 50 yards, it was discouraging to see Williams underwhelm with just three catches for 25 yards this week. I'm still holding out hope though.

The rookie continues to play the most snaps and run the most routes in the Commanders' receiver room. This was the third straight outing where Williams accomplished that feat, following Terry McLaurin's season-ending injury.

It's possible Williams levels off as a low-ceiling WR4/flex option for fantasy, but I'm holding for at least another week. The talent is evident and Washington has one of the top-10 easiest schedules for fantasy receivers the rest of the way.

It's easy to forget, but Bryant had a growing role in the Broncos' passing attack during the opening month before being placed on injured reserve with an ankle injury. In those first four games, Bryant topped 40 yards three times.

So, it was great to see him post a season-high 56 yards and a touchdown on four catches in his first game back since the injury. Jaylen Waddle also suffered a concussion, but he should be cleared after their Week 10 bye.

Regardless, Bryant was a borderline flex option early in the season and has returned with an even better performance. Given Waddle's struggles and Courtland Sutton's age, there's a non-zero chance Bryant becomes the most valuable fantasy receiver in Denver for the rest of the season.

Watkins' fantasy value is difficult to assess because it's entirely dependent on the health of the other receivers in San Francisco.

Deebo Samuel Sr. is nearing a return from a hamstring injury that kept him out for the last month. Mike Evans' Achilles tendinitis is an ongoing problem with no clear recovery timeline. I'll be surprised if he suits up in Week 10. Demarcus Robinson is apparently playing at less than 100%, while De'Zhaun Stribling and Christian Kirk are on IR.

That opened the door for Watkins, who put up four receptions, 86 yards and a touchdown versus the Raiders. That's his second consecutive quality stat line, after a 56-yard performance versus the Falcons prior to the Niners' Week 8 bye.

If everything remains the same with Evans and Deebo sitting out, Watkins will be a very enticing WR4/flex option in a good matchup with the Cowboys this week.

Jordyn Tyson is 54% rostered but would be the No. 1 WR add, if available.

Makai Lemon is 53% rostered but would be the No. 3 WR add behind Tyson and D.Douglas, if available.

Courtland Sutton is 53% rostered but would be the No. 5 WR add behind Tyson, D. Douglas, Lemon, and C. Douglas, if available.

Khalil Shakir is 53% rostered but would be the No. 8 WR add behind Tyson, D. Douglas, Lemon, C. Douglas, Williams, Sutton and Bryant, if available.

Mack Hollins vs. DET (Germany) (5% rostered)

Efton Chism III vs. DET (Germany) (2% rostered)

Dart-throw flex options (if starter is out)

In the four games since Terry McLaurin's season-ending injury, Okonkwo has posted stat lines for 4-60-0, 7-69-0, 2-32-0 and 6-57-0.

While he hasn't found the end zone, that type of volume (at least six targets in three of the four appearances) makes him the 10th-most targeted tight end on a per game basis during that stretch.

Okonkwo has moved into the high-end TE2 streamer conversation, with a chance to crack the low-end TE1 range moving forward.

Puka Nacua is heading to IR, Davante Adams is already there and Terrance Ferguson isn't expected back for another couple weeks.

Higbee has disappeared from fantasy relevance following his impressive three-game stretch from Week 3 to Week 5, when he posted 8-62-1, 5-67-0 and 5-83-0. Since then, the veteran has been held to 22 yards or less in four straight contests and hasn't found the end zone.

However, the Rams will have no choice but to increase his targets with Nacua out. That puts Higbee back in the mix as a TE2 streamer with upside.

Eddy Pineiro is 54% rostered but would be the No. 1 K add, if available.

Tyler Bass is 57% rostered but would be the No. 2 K add behind Pinerio, if available.

Quarterbacks: Lamar Jackson, Kyler Murray, Justin Herbert

Running Backs: J.K. Dobbins, Emanuel Wilson

Lamar Jackson is undergoing surgery that will sideline him for 6-10 weeks. If you're stashing him in hopes that he'll return for the fantasy playoffs, you're wasting a roster spot.

Despite the Texans making a trade for Marvin Harrison Jr., he's just part of a big rotation of receivers behind Nico Collins. It's possible MHJ could carve out a bigger role as the season progresses, but it's highly unlikely he'll make a major fantasy impact — unless Collins gets hurt. Even in that scenario, I'm not confident Harrison would step up.

With Jordyn Tyson now in the lineup, Devaughn Vele's days as a fantasy flex option are over.
