#2765. Manchester United coach Michael Carrick ‘expected’ to be fired after rough start

Manchester United coach Michael Carrick 'expected' to be fired after rough start appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

Currently in the Premier League's relegation zone, Manchester United have won just one league game so far this season. Their eight points put them above Sunderland on goal difference alone, while both clubs are only one point ahead of last-place Crystal Palace. After saving the Red Devils in an interim role last year, head coach Michael Carrick had the label removed after the club's finish following former manager Ruben Amorim's firing.

Now, it appears that Carrick could be joining him. According to The Athletic's David Ornstein, Adam Crafton, and Laurie Whitwell, Manchester United is expected to fire Carrick before this current international break ends.

"Manchester United are expected to relieve Michael Carrick of his duties as head coach," wrote the trio on Monday afternoon.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

United's 1-5-4 record and -5 goal differential are just the start of the club's woes so far this fall. Following another busy summer transfer window in which the Red Devils' midfield was rebuilt with three signings, expectations were high at Old Trafford. Many believed that Manchester United would not only challenge for the Premier League title, but once again compete in European football as well.

However, Carrick has failed to deliver on those goals. Manchester United has only beaten Ipswich Town at home in the Premier League, and that was on August 30th. They've only won two of their four Champions League matchups, most recently against Roma at home last week. A home victory over Sabah on September 10th is the only other victory for the Red Devils this fall.

After a disappointing start filled with heartbreak and questionable decision making, it makes sense that the Manchester United brass would look to make a change. Owners Jim Ratcliffe and the Glazer Family, along with other key team leaders like team CEO Omar Berrada, will make the ultimate call on Carrick's future over the current international break.

By the time that United return to the pitch on November 22nd against Liverpool at Anfield, a new head coach could be roaming the visiting sidelines. Will Carrick's potential replacement lead Manchester United back to the promised land? Or will it be another failed appointment, like Carrick, Amorim and Erik ten Hag were over the last three years?

#2764. Kevin Stefanski on Jessie Bates trade: Falcons aren't punting on the season

The Falcons traded veteran safety Jessie Bates to the Jaguars today, which some might see as a sign that the Falcons don’t see themselves as contenders and are looking ahead at 2027. Falcons head coach Kevin Stefanski says that’s not the case.

Stefanski did acknowledge that the Falcons are building for the future, but he also noted that in the NFC South, Atlanta remains very much in contention at 3-6.

“You guys know how I feel about Jessie the person, Jessie the player,” Stefanski told reporters. “But at the same time, this is football, where sometimes you have one foot in the short term, one foot in the long term. There’s things like this that make your team better in the long run. Draft picks are the life blood of an organization. That’s something we believe in.

Stefanski said he wants fans to know that they still are going to compete to win the NFC South.

“Our fans are smart enough to know that in no way, shape or form would we punt on this season. We have three wins, just like the rest of our division. Obviously not where we want to be, we want to play better, but we’re in the thick of this thing and we trust in the guys we have,” Stefanski said.

The Falcons might not be punting on the season, but they are deciding to move on from a player who can help them this season and get draft picks for future seasons. The Falcons still might win the worst division in football, but today’s trade is not the trade of a team that thinks it’s a Super Bowl contender this year.

#2763. Lakers getting Luka Dončić back, but other big three member status uncertain

ATLANTA — The Lakers are getting their superstar guard back for Monday's road game against the Hawks.

But the status of his backcourt co-star was uncertain as of six hours before tipoff.

Luka Doncic scored a season-high 53 points in Friday's win over the Trail Blazers before sitting out of the Lakers' win over the Pistons on Sunday. AP Photo/Mark J. Terrill

Luka Dončić was taken off of the injury report ahead of the Lakers' matchup against the Hawks after sitting out of Sunday's 121-116 win over the Pistons because of low back tightness.

Austin Reaves stepped up in light of Dončić's absence, scoring a season-high 43 points to lead the Lakers to their fifth straight win and putting them at 9-2 entering Monday.

Austin Reaves stepped up against the Pistons in light of Luka Doncic not being available. Getty Images

Reaves was listed as questionable on the team's injury report against the Hawks because of an illness.

Quentin Grimes, who started in place of Dončić against the Pistons, was ruled out against the Hawks because of a sprained finger on his right hand.

Quentin Grimes seems to have avoided a major injury. NBAE via Getty Images

Coach JJ Redick said Grimes banged his finger during the game agains the Pistons, leading to the 6-foot-5 guard going back to the locker room during the fourth quarter on Sunday with a member of the medical staff. Redick said Grimes' X-rays came back negative.

Dončić is the league's leading scorer, averaging 40.1 points per game entering Monday.

#2762. Vikings officially sign Odell Beckham Jr. to active roster

Wide receiver Odell Beckham Jr. is officially set to make his Vikings debut against the Bills on Monday night.

Word that the Vikings would sign Beckham to their 53-man roster surfaced on Sunday and the team announced the move on Monday afternoon. Beckham has been on the practice squad for the last couple of weeks and was elevated to the active roster once without appearing in a game.

Beckham played in three games for the Giants in September, but was released after going without a catch in those appearances. He did not play in 2025, so he’ll be looking for his first regular season catch since he was with the Dolphins in Week 14 of the 2024 season.

The Vikings placed running back Demond Claiborne on injured reserve in a corresponding move. They also elevated safety Tavierre Thomas from the practice squad.
